h\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
, where
A_{a,z_{0},j}(h)
is the
D\times s
matrix whose
l
-th column is equal to
x_{l}-z_{0}
if
l\neq j
and whose
j
-th column is
P_{[i],x_{j},z_{0}}(h)-h
, with
P_{[i],x_{j},z_{0}}
being the differential at
z_{0}
of the projection on a small neighborhood of
x_{j}
in
{\mathsf{M}}
(as in the statement of Lemma
4.3
).
This projection coincides with
p_{j}:B(z_{0},\delta)\rightarrow B(x_{j},\delta^{\prime})\cap{\mathsf{M}}
,
hence by definition of the form
B
from Equation
25
in Lemma
6.7
we have
B(h)=B_{a,z_{0}}(h)
for all
h\in E^{\perp}
with
\|h\|<\overline{\delta}
for some
0<\overline{\delta}<\delta
.
By applying Corollary
6.8
and using the same reasoning for each critical point of
{\mathsf{M}}
, this is enough to conclude that
{\mathsf{M}}
satisfies Condition
(P4)
.
∎

9.4
Density of the transversality conditions

In the previous subsection, we defined five sets in the space of multijets that can be written as finite unions of closed Whitney stratified sets, and shown that the transversality to those sets of the map
j^{r}_{s}(i):M^{(s)}\rightarrow J^{r}_{s}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
(respectively the map
(j^{r}_{s})_{M}\rho_{i}:M^{(s)}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\rightarrow J^{r}_{s}(M,{\mathbb{R}})
) induced by an embedding
i:M\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
implies that
i(M)
satisfies the conditions
(P1)
-
(P4)
that are of interest to us.
9
9

9

The property of transversality must be verified for either
s=2,\ldots,D+2
,
s=2,\ldots,D+1
or
s=3,\ldots,D+1
depending on the set considered.

It remains to show that the set of embeddings for which these properties of transversality are satisfied is dense in the Whitney topology; with this, we will have proved the second half of the Genericity Theorem
1.1
, as well as Remark
1.3
.
To that end, the transversality theorems presented in Subsection
9.2
are the key ingredients.

Theorem 9.25

(The Density Theorem)
.

Let
M
be a compact
C^{k}
manifold for some
k\in{\mathbb{N}}\cup\{\infty\}
with
k\geq 1
.
Then, the set of
C^{k}
embeddings
i:M\to{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
such that
{\mathsf{M}}=i(M)
satisfies conditions
(P1)
,
(P2)
,
(P3)
and
(P4)
is dense in
\text{Emb}^{k}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
for the Whitney
C^{k}
-topology.

Proof.

The only difficulty here is that the transversality theorems
9.1
and
9.3
only state results regarding the density of
C^{r+1}
functions whose multijets are transverse to sets in
J^{r}_{s}(M,{\mathbb{R}})
and
J^{r}_{s}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
, as opposed to
C^{r}
functions, and relatedly, we had to assume throughout Subsection
9.3
that
M
was a
C^{k}
manifold for
k\geq 3
.
Hence we have to be somewhat cautious to account for the cases
k=1,2
, as proceeding naively would force us to ask that
M
be at least
C^{3}
. The key ingredient here will be that
C^{r_{1}}
maps are dense in
C^{r_{2}}
maps for
r_{1}\geq r_{2}
and for the Whitney
C^{r_{2}}
-topology.

Throughout the proof, the sets
W_{0}^{(s)},W_{1}^{(s)},W_{2}^{(s)},W_{3}^{(s)}
and
W_{4}^{(s)}
are as defined in Subsection
9.3
.
Let us start by assuming that
k\geq 3
.
For
r=2
and a given
s=2,\ldots,D+2
, the set
W_{0}^{(s)}\subset J^{2}_{s}(M)
is a closed submanifold (hence in particular a closed Whitney stratifiable set). As
k\geq r+1=3
, we can apply the transversality theorem adapted to the case of distance functions (
9.3
) to
W_{0}^{(s)}
(simply with
A=M^{(s)}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
, in the notations of the theorem) to get that the set

F_{W_{0}^{(s)}}:=\{f\in C^{k}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D}):\;(j^{2}_{s})_{M}\rho_{f}\text{ is transverse to }W_{0}^{(s)}\text{ in }J_{s}^{2}(M,{\mathbb{R}})\}

is residual in
C^{k}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
(note that we have not yet restricted ourselves to embeddings).
Similarly, we have shown in Lemmas
9.10
,
9.14
,
9.18
and
9.23
that
W_{1}^{(s)},W_{2}^{(s)},W_{3}^{(s)},W_{4}^{(s)}\subset J^{2}_{s}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
are finite unions of closed Whitney stratifiable subsets
\{W_{2,\alpha}^{(s)}\}_{\alpha\in A}
,
\{W_{3,\beta}^{(s)}\}_{\beta\in B}
,
\{W_{1,\gamma}^{(s)}\}_{\gamma\in C}
and
\{W_{4,\nu}^{(s)}\}_{\nu\in N}
respectively.
The transversality theorem
9.1
(with
A=M^{(s)}
) then yields that for any
s=2,\ldots,D+1
and
\alpha\in A
, the set

F_{W_{2,\alpha}^{(s)}}:=\{f\in C^{k}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D}):\;j^{2}_{s}(f)\text{ is transverse to }W_{2,\alpha}^{(s)}\text{ in }J_{s}^{2}(M,{\mathbb{R}})\}

is residual in
C^{k}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
, and similarly for the sets

F_{W_{3,\beta}^{(s)}}:=\{f\in C^{k}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D}):\;j^{2}_{s}(f)\text{ is transverse to }W_{3,\beta}^{(s)}\text{ in }J_{s}^{2}(M,{\mathbb{R}})\}

and

F_{W_{4,\nu}^{(s)}}:=\{f\in C^{k}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D}):\;j^{2}_{s}(f)\text{ is transverse to }W_{4,\nu}^{(s)}\text{ in }J_{s}^{2}(M_{k},{\mathbb{R}})\}

for
\beta\in B
,
\nu\in N
and
s=2,\ldots,D+1
and the sets

F_{W_{1,\gamma}^{(s)}}:=\{f\in C^{k}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D}):\;j^{2}_{s}(f)\text{ is transverse to }W_{1,\gamma}^{(s)}\text{ in }J_{s}^{2}(M,{\mathbb{R}})\}

for
\gamma\in C
and
s=3,\ldots,D+1
.
As
C^{k}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
is a Baire space (see e.g.
[
Hir94
, Thm 4.4]
), the (finite) intersection

F:=\bigcap_{s=2}^{D+2}F_{W_{0}^{(s)}}\cap\bigcap_{s=3}^{D+1}\left(\bigcap_{\gamma\in C}F_{W_{1,\gamma}^{(s)}}\right)\cap\bigcap_{s=2}^{D+1}\left(\bigcap_{\alpha\in A}F_{W_{2,\alpha}^{(s)}}\cap\bigcap_{\beta\in B}F_{W_{3,\beta}^{(s)}}\cap\bigcap_{\nu\in N}F_{W_{4,\nu}^{(s)}}\right)

of residual sets is yet again residual, and in particular dense in
C^{k}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
.
As the set of embeddings
\text{Emb}^{k}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
is open in
C^{k}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
(see
[
Mic80
, Prop 5.3]
), the set
E:=F\cap\text{Emb}^{k}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
is dense in
\text{Emb}^{k}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
.

Let
i\in E
; then
(j^{2}_{s})_{M}\rho_{i}
is transverse to
W_{0}^{(s)}
for
s=2,\ldots,D+2
, hence we know from Yomdin’s Lemma
9.19
that
i(M)
satisfies Condition
(P0)
. From Lemma
9.15
(which we can apply, as we have assumed that
i
is
C^{k}
with
k\geq 3=r+1
), and as
j^{2}_{s}(i)
is transverse to the sets
\{W_{2,\alpha}^{(s)}\}_{\alpha\in A}
and
\{W_{3,\beta}^{(s)}\}_{\beta\in B}
for
s=2,\ldots,D+1
, we get that
i(M)
satisfies conditions
(P2)
and
(P3)
.
This, in addition to the transversality of
j^{2}_{s}(i)
to
\{W_{1,\gamma}^{(s)}\}_{\gamma\in C}
for
s=3,\ldots,D+1
and to
\{W_{4,\nu}^{(s)}\}_{\nu\in N}
for
s=2,\ldots,D+1
, allows us to apply Lemmas
9.19
and
9.24
(again,
i
is at least
C^{3}
) to get that
i(M)
also fulfills Conditions
(P1)
and
(P4)
.
Hence we have shown that the
C^{k}
embeddings that satisfy conditions
(P1)
-
(P4)
are dense in
\text{Emb}^{k}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
for the Whitney
C^{k}
-topology, which completes the proof when
k\geq 3
.

Assume now that
k\in\{1,2\}
.
Using Theorem 2.9 from
[
Hir94
, Chapter 2]
, we can equip
M
with a
C^{3}
differential structure that is compatible with its given
C^{k}
differential structure. To make a clear distinction, we write
M_{3}
to refer to
M
equipped with this structure.
Let
U\subset\text{Emb}^{k}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})=\text{Emb}^{k}(M_{3},{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
be any non-empty open set of the Whitney
C^{k}
-topology.
As the
C^{3}
embeddings
\text{Emb}^{3}(M_{3},{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
seen as a subset of
\text{Emb}^{k}(M_{3},{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
are dense in
\text{Emb}^{k}(M_{3},{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
for the Whitney
C^{k}
-topology (see e.g.
[
Hir94
, Section 2, Thm 2.7]
), there is a
C^{3}
embedding
i\in U\cap\text{Emb}^{3}(M_{3},{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
.
As we have shown above that the
C^{3}
embeddings
i^{\prime}
such that
i^{\prime}(M)
satisfies Conditions
(P1-4)
are dense in
\text{Emb}^{3}(M_{3},{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
for the Whitney
C^{3}
-topology, we can find such embeddings arbitrarily close to
i
for the
C^{3}
-norm, hence also for the coarser
C^{k}
-norm. Such an embedding
i^{\prime}
is in particular a
C^{k}
embedding for the initial structure on
M
, and if
\|i-i^{\prime}\|_{C^{k}}
is small enough, then
i^{\prime}
must belong to
U
.
Hence we can find
i^{\prime}\in U
such that
i^{\prime}(M)
satisfies Conditions
(P1-4)
, which is enough to conclude.
∎

Remark 9.26
.

The observant reader might wonder why we did not use the “openness” part of the transversality theorems
9.1
and
9.3
to directly get that the set of
C^{k}
embeddings
i:M\to{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
such that
i(M)
satisfies conditions
(P1)
,
(P2)
,
(P3)
and
(P4)
is open in
\text{Emb}^{k}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
for the Whitney
C^{k}
-topology.
The even more observant reader remembers that Theorem
9.1
guarantees that if
k\geq r+1
, the set of
C^{k}
embeddings
i
such that
j^{r}_{s}(i)
is transverse on
A\subset M^{(s)}
to some closed Whitney stratified set
W\subset J_{s}^{r}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
is open in the Whitney
C^{r+1}
-topology under the condition that
A
be compact (and similarly for Theorem
9.3
).
Here we face two problems: the first is that we need transversality on
A=M^{(s)}
, which is not compact.
This hurdle can in fact be avoided by controlling the distance between the projections of critical points, though some work is required.
The second problem is that for our sets
W\subset J_{s}^{r}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
to be defined, we need
r\geq 2
, hence
k\geq 3
, and we could not find a way around this limitation;
hence, though we could have shown that the set of suitable embeddings is open in the Whitney
C^{3}
-topology using the two transversality theorems, we needed the more concrete and geometric proof of the
C^{2}
Stability Theorem
1.4
to show that this set is also open in the Whitney
C^{2}
-topology.

As a corollary of the Density Theorem and the
C^{2}
Stability Theorem
1.4
, we finally get the Genericity Theorem
1.1
.

Proof of the Genericity Theorem
1.1
.

The density part of the statement comes directly from the Density Theorem
9.25
.
From the
C^{2}
Stability Theorem
1.4
, we get that the set of embeddings
i\in\mathrm{Emb}^{2}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
such that
i(M)
satisfies
(P1)
,
(P2)
,
(P3)
and
(P4)
is open in
\mathrm{Emb}^{2}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
for the Whitney
C^{2}
-topology. As the Whitney
C^{2}
-topology on
\mathrm{Emb}^{k}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})\subset\mathrm{Emb}^{2}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
is the subspace topology inherited from the Whitney
C^{2}
-topology on
\mathrm{Emb}^{2}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
, the set of
C^{k}
embeddings such that
i(M)
satisfies
(P1)
,
(P2)
,
(P3)
and
(P4)
is also open in
\mathrm{Emb}^{k}(M,{\mathbb{R}}^{D})
.
∎
